People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I came in because things at my apartment have gotten… I don’t know, it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making a big deal out of it, but I know people there are watching when I come and go. Like, they notice what time I leave, what time I come back, who I talk to, that kind of thing. And then there’s these notes, or papers, on the doors and around the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Oh, those are just normal notices,” like building notices or whatever, but it doesn’t feel normal to me.

It feels like some of them are about me, or that people are slipping notes to the office about me. Like they’re keeping track. I can tell by the way things show up after I’ve done something, or how people act when I walk by. I know they’ll say it’s nothing, but it’s been bothering me enough that I figured I better come talk to somebody before it gets worse or before they twist it around more.

I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think so in the way people usually mean it. Like, I’m not sitting in my apartment hearing somebody talking clear as day when nobody’s there, if that’s what you mean.

Unusually high… no, I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not really the type to get all energized or excited like that, if that’s what you mean. apartment checking the window over and over.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: Bills and not_in_workforce / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because things at my apartment have gotten… I don’t know, it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making a big deal out of it, but I know people there are watching when I come and go. Like, they notice what time I leave, what time I come back, who I talk to, that kind of thing. And then there’s these notes, or papers, on the doors and around the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Oh, those are just normal notices,” like building notices or whatever, but it doesn’t feel normal to me.\n\nIt feels like some of them are about me, or that people are slipping notes to the office about me. Like, I’m not sitting in my apartment hearing somebody talking clear as day when nobody’s there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I hear noises in the building, people in the hallway, doors, voices through the walls maybe, but it’s hard to tell in apartments because sound carries weird. So I don’t want to say “voices” like it’s coming out of nowhere, because I’m not sure that’s what’s happening. It’s more that I notice things and I wonder what people are saying, but as far as hearing a voice when no one else is around… no, not really that I can say.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was unusually high or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Unusually high… no, I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not really the type to get all energized or excited like that, if that’s what you mean. I can get short with people, or I’ll feel on edge, but I don’t know if that’s “unusual” exactly.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is. I don’t have times where I feel on top of the world or like I don’t need to slow down. Mostly it’s more like I’m bothered, tense, worn out. If I seem irritable, it’s usually because something’s been building up and I’m trying to figure out what’s going on.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Yeah… yeah, I think so. Most days it’s there, especially in the morning. I wake up and before I even really do anything, before I even get my feet on the floor, there’s this heavy kind of hollow feeling. Like the day already went bad somehow, even though it just started.

It’s hard to explain because it’s not always like I’m crying or anything dramatic. It’s more like this weight sitting in me, and I’m already tired of the day before I’ve even started it. Some mornings I just lie there for a bit, thinking, “Here we go again,” and it feels like I’m dragging myself into the day instead of just getting up like a normal person. So, yeah… depressed most of the day, a lot of days, that sounds about right.

It’s more that I notice things and I wonder what people are saying, but as far as hearing a voice when no one else is around… no, not really that I can say.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was unusually high or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Unusually high… no, I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not really the type to get all energized or excited like that, if that’s what you mean. Irritable, maybe sometimes, but I mean, who wouldn’t be when things feel off around you or people are acting strange? I can get short with people, or I’ll feel on edge, but I don’t know if that’s “unusual” exactly.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is. I don’t have times where I feel on top of the world or like I don’t need to slow down. Mostly it’s more like I’m bothered, tense, worn out. If I seem irritable, it’s usually because something’s been building up and I’m trying to figure out what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. Most days it’s there, especially in the morning. I wake up and before I even really do anything, before I even get my feet on the floor, there’s this heavy kind of hollow feeling. Like the day already went bad somehow, even though it just started.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because it’s not always like I’m crying or anything dramatic. It’s more like this weight sitting in me, and I’m already tired of the day before I’ve even started it. Some mornings I just lie there for a bit, thinking, “Here we go again,” and it feels like I’m dragging myself into the day instead of just getting up like a normal person. So, yeah… depressed most of the day, a lot of days, that sounds about right.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had the feeling that your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say it like that, like somebody is putting thoughts inside my head. That sounds… I don’t know, like a very specific thing. What I do know is I came in because something is going on at my apartment, and everybody keeps trying to make it sound like it’s nothing.

The people there are watching when I leave and when I come back. I can tell because it’s too lined up, you know? Like I’ll go out, and then there’ll be notes on doors, or something slipped around, and they say, “Oh, those are just notices from the office,” like it’s normal building stuff. But it’s not just that. It feels like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings and then passing notes about me to the office, and then when I say it, people act like I’m making too much of it.

So… thoughts being put in my mind, no, I wouldn’t describe it that way. But I do feel like other people are involved in what’s happening around me, and they know more about me than they should.

It’s been… I’d say about a year, a little more than a year. Maybe around a year and a month, if I’m trying to place it right.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, like, “this started first and then that started,” because in my head they kind of feed into each other. But what I notice is when I’m really down — like in that hollow place I was talking about, where I’m barely eating, not really picking up the phone, just sitting there with the curtains closed and not wanting to deal with anybody — that’s when I’m also the most sure about the apartment people.

Like, those are the times I feel it the strongest, that they’re tracking when I come and go, talking to the office, keeping notes or whatever they’re doing. When I’m lower, it all feels more obvious to me, like I can see the pattern clearer, even if other people tell me I’m reading too much into it.

So… I don’t know if I can say for sure the depression came before the apartment problems. I just know when the depression gets bad, that’s when the apartment stuff feels the most real and the most intense.

But then it kept lining up, over and over, with the neighbors and the office, and I started realizing it wasn’t just a bad week or something.\n\nSo yeah… these thoughts about the apartment, people watching and passing things along, that’s been going on for about a year and a month now.\n\nDoctor: When the depression first started, was it before the problems at your apartment began?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, like, “this started first and then that started,” because in my head they kind of feed into each other. But what I notice is when I’m really down — like in that hollow place I was talking about, where I’m barely eating, not really picking up the phone, just sitting there with the curtains closed and not wanting to deal with anybody — that’s when I’m also the most sure about the apartment people.\n\nLike, those are the times I feel it the strongest, that they’re tracking when I come and go, talking to the office, keeping notes or whatever they’re doing. When I’m lower, it all feels more obvious to me, like I can see the pattern clearer, even if other people tell me I’m reading too much into it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I can say for sure the depression came before the apartment problems. I just know when the depression gets bad, that’s when the apartment stuff feels the most real and the most intense.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave there been times when the apartment concerns were present even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yes, there have been times like that. That’s part of what makes it hard for me when people say it’s only because I’m depressed, because there was a stretch — not a long, perfect stretch, but several weeks — where I was doing a little better. I was eating more normal, or at least better than I had been, and I wasn’t waking up with that same heavy hopeless feeling right away every morning.

But even then, I still felt like the neighbors were watching my door. I’d still notice when people were out in the hallway, or when things seemed to happen right after I left or came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like feeling a little better made that go away. Maybe I wasn’t as crushed down inside during that time, but I still thought the apartment stuff was happening.

most of the time since I moved, even on days when I could sound a little clearer, I still felt low, useless, and uninterested in the things I used to do.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a spell of several weeks when I was eating a bit better and not waking up so hopeless, but I still believed the neighbors were watching my door and reporting on me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I have fallen behind on bills, missed appointments, and stopped showing up for church and volunteer commitments because I cannot keep my days organized or trust ordinary interactions.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about a year and five months, since I left the house I shared with my wife and moved into the senior apartment.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The suspicious thoughts about the apartment neighbors and office have been with me for about a year and a month.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The heavy, no-point feeling and pulling away from things have been going on for about a year and two months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 59 / not_in_workforce / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: How long have these experiences at your apartment been going on?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about a year, a little more than a year. Maybe around a year and a month, if I’m trying to place it right.\n\nIt didn’t all come on at once like one big event. At first it was just little things I noticed, and I thought maybe I was just being careful or maybe the building was busy. But then it kept lining up, over and over, with the neighbors and the office, and I started realizing it wasn’t just a bad week or something.\n\nSo yeah… these thoughts about the apartment, people watching and passing things along, that’s been going on for about a year and a month now.\n\nDoctor: When the depression first started, was it before the problems at your apartment began?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, like, “this started first and then that started,” because in my head they kind of feed into each other. But what I notice is when I’m really down — like in that hollow place I was talking about, where I’m barely eating, not really picking up the phone, just sitting there with the curtains closed and not wanting to deal with anybody — that’s when I’m also the most sure about the apartment people.\n\nLike, those are the times I feel it the strongest, that they’re tracking when I come and go, talking to the office, keeping notes or whatever they’re doing. When I’m lower, it all feels more obvious to me, like I can see the pattern clearer, even if other people tell me I’m reading too much into it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I can say for sure the depression came before the apartment problems. I just know when the depression gets bad, that’s when the apartment stuff feels the most real and the most intense.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the apartment concerns were present even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, there have been times like that. That’s part of what makes it hard for me when people say it’s only because I’m depressed, because there was a stretch — not a long, perfect stretch, but several weeks — where I was doing a little better. I was eating more normal, or at least better than I had been, and I wasn’t waking up with that same heavy hopeless feeling right away every morning.\n\nBut even then, I still felt like the neighbors were watching my door. I’d still notice when people were out in the hallway, or when things seemed to happen right after I left or came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like feeling a little better made that go away. Maybe I wasn’t as crushed down inside during that time, but I still thought the apartment stuff was happening.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you’re feeling depressed, do you ever believe that you’re to blame for bad things happening, even when other people say that isn’t true?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d put it exactly like I’m to blame for bad things happening. It’s more like… when I’m depressed, I get even more stuck on what’s going on at my apartment. That’s really why I came in, because I know the people there are watching when I leave and when I come back, like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings.

And then there are these notes — people keep telling me, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like regular apartment notices, but it doesn’t feel like that to me. It feels like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like they’re passing along information. Everybody says I’m making too much of it, but I can’t just ignore it when it keeps happening.

So… I don’t know about being the cause of bad things, exactly. It’s more that I feel targeted, like they’re watching me and reporting on me, even when other people keep saying it isn’t true.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n9\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F20.0, F25.1, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because things at my apartment have gotten… I don’t know, it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making a big deal out of it, but I know people there are watching when I come and go. Like, they notice what time I leave, what time I come back, who I talk to, that kind of thing. And then there’s these notes, or papers, on the doors and around the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Oh, those are just normal notices,” like building notices or whatever, but it doesn’t feel normal to me.\n\nIt feels like some of them are about me, or that people are slipping notes to the office about me. Like they’re keeping track. I can tell by the way things show up after I’ve done something, or how people act when I walk by. I know they’ll say it’s nothing, but it’s been bothering me enough that I figured I better come talk to somebody before it gets worse or before they twist it around more.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think so in the way people usually mean it. Like, I’m not sitting in my apartment hearing somebody talking clear as day when nobody’s there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I hear noises in the building, people in the hallway, doors, voices through the walls maybe, but it’s hard to tell in apartments because sound carries weird. So I don’t want to say “voices” like it’s coming out of nowhere, because I’m not sure that’s what’s happening. It’s more that I notice things and I wonder what people are saying, but as far as hearing a voice when no one else is around… no, not really that I can say.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was unusually high or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Unusually high… no, I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not really the type to get all energized or excited like that, if that’s what you mean. Irritable, maybe sometimes, but I mean, who wouldn’t be when things feel off around you or people are acting strange? I can get short with people, or I’ll feel on edge, but I don’t know if that’s “unusual” exactly.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is. I don’t have times where I feel on top of the world or like I don’t need to slow down. Mostly it’s more like I’m bothered, tense, worn out. If I seem irritable, it’s usually because something’s been building up and I’m trying to figure out what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. Most days it’s there, especially in the morning. I wake up and before I even really do anything, before I even get my feet on the floor, there’s this heavy kind of hollow feeling. Like the day already went bad somehow, even though it just started.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because it’s not always like I’m crying or anything dramatic. It’s more like this weight sitting in me, and I’m already tired of the day before I’ve even started it. Some mornings I just lie there for a bit, thinking, “Here we go again,” and it feels like I’m dragging myself into the day instead of just getting up like a normal person. So, yeah… depressed most of the day, a lot of days, that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Have you had the feeling that your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it like that, like somebody is putting thoughts inside my head. That sounds… I don’t know, like a very specific thing. What I do know is I came in because something is going on at my apartment, and everybody keeps trying to make it sound like it’s nothing.\n\nThe people there are watching when I leave and when I come back. I can tell because it’s too lined up, you know? Like I’ll go out, and then there’ll be notes on doors, or something slipped around, and they say, “Oh, those are just notices from the office,” like it’s normal building stuff. But it’s not just that. It feels like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings and then passing notes about me to the office, and then when I say it, people act like I’m making too much of it.\n\nSo… thoughts being put in my mind, no, I wouldn’t describe it that way. But I do feel like other people are involved in what’s happening around me, and they know more about me than they should.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences at your apartment been going on?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about a year, a little more than a year. Maybe around a year and a month, if I’m trying to place it right.\n\nIt didn’t all come on at once like one big event. At first it was just little things I noticed, and I thought maybe I was just being careful or maybe the building was busy. But then it kept lining up, over and over, with the neighbors and the office, and I started realizing it wasn’t just a bad week or something.\n\nSo yeah… these thoughts about the apartment, people watching and passing things along, that’s been going on for about a year and a month now.\n\nDoctor: When the depression first started, was it before the problems at your apartment began?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, like, “this started first and then that started,” because in my head they kind of feed into each other. But what I notice is when I’m really down — like in that hollow place I was talking about, where I’m barely eating, not really picking up the phone, just sitting there with the curtains closed and not wanting to deal with anybody — that’s when I’m also the most sure about the apartment people.\n\nLike, those are the times I feel it the strongest, that they’re tracking when I come and go, talking to the office, keeping notes or whatever they’re doing. When I’m lower, it all feels more obvious to me, like I can see the pattern clearer, even if other people tell me I’m reading too much into it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I can say for sure the depression came before the apartment problems. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… there was. I mean, I can’t give you exact dates like on a calendar, because I don’t keep track that neatly, but there was a spell that went on for several weeks where I wasn’t as depressed as I had been.

Like, I was eating a little better — not perfect or anything, but more regular — and I wasn’t waking up every morning with that same hopeless feeling sitting on my chest right away. I could get up a little easier, do a few things, maybe answer the phone once in a while.

But the apartment thing didn’t stop during that time. I still felt sure the neighbors were watching my door, watching when I left and came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like my mood got a little lighter and then I suddenly thought, “Oh, that’s nothing.” I still thought it was happening. So yes, I’d say it lasted at least a couple weeks, probably several weeks, even when I wasn’t feeling that depressed.

I know they’ll say it’s nothing, but it’s been bothering me enough that I figured I better come talk to somebody before it gets worse or before they twist it around more.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think so in the way people usually mean it. Like, I’m not sitting in my apartment hearing somebody talking clear as day when nobody’s there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I hear noises in the building, people in the hallway, doors, voices through the walls maybe, but it’s hard to tell in apartments because sound carries weird. So I don’t want to say “voices” like it’s coming out of nowhere, because I’m not sure that’s what’s happening. It’s more that I notice things and I wonder what people are saying, but as far as hearing a voice when no one else is around… no, not really that I can say.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was unusually high or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Unusually high… no, I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not really the type to get all energized or excited like that, if that’s what you mean. Irritable, maybe sometimes, but I mean, who wouldn’t be when things feel off around you or people are acting strange? I can get short with people, or I’ll feel on edge, but I don’t know if that’s “unusual” exactly.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is. I don’t have times where I feel on top of the world or like I don’t need to slow down. Mostly it’s more like I’m bothered, tense, worn out. If I seem irritable, it’s usually because something’s been building up and I’m trying to figure out what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. Most days it’s there, especially in the morning. I wake up and before I even really do anything, before I even get my feet on the floor, there’s this heavy kind of hollow feeling. Like the day already went bad somehow, even though it just started.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because it’s not always like I’m crying or anything dramatic. It’s more like this weight sitting in me, and I’m already tired of the day before I’ve even started it. Some mornings I just lie there for a bit, thinking, “Here we go again,” and it feels like I’m dragging myself into the day instead of just getting up like a normal person. So, yeah… depressed most of the day, a lot of days, that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Have you had the feeling that your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it like that, like somebody is putting thoughts inside my head. That sounds… I don’t know, like a very specific thing. What I do know is I came in because something is going on at my apartment, and everybody keeps trying to make it sound like it’s nothing.\n\nThe people there are watching when I leave and when I come back. I can tell because it’s too lined up, you know? Like I’ll go out, and then there’ll be notes on doors, or something slipped around, and they say, “Oh, those are just notices from the office,” like it’s normal building stuff. But it’s not just that. It feels like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings and then passing notes about me to the office, and then when I say it, people act like I’m making too much of it.\n\nSo… thoughts being put in my mind, no, I wouldn’t describe it that way. But I do feel like other people are involved in what’s happening around me, and they know more about me than they should.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences at your apartment been going on?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about a year, a little more than a year. Maybe around a year and a month, if I’m trying to place it right.\n\nIt didn’t all come on at once like one big event. At first it was just little things I noticed, and I thought maybe I was just being careful or maybe the building was busy. But then it kept lining up, over and over, with the neighbors and the office, and I started realizing it wasn’t just a bad week or something.\n\nSo yeah… these thoughts about the apartment, people watching and passing things along, that’s been going on for about a year and a month now.\n\nDoctor: When the depression first started, was it before the problems at your apartment began?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, like, “this started first and then that started,” because in my head they kind of feed into each other. But what I notice is when I’m really down — like in that hollow place I was talking about, where I’m barely eating, not really picking up the phone, just sitting there with the curtains closed and not wanting to deal with anybody — that’s when I’m also the most sure about the apartment people.\n\nLike, those are the times I feel it the strongest, that they’re tracking when I come and go, talking to the office, keeping notes or whatever they’re doing. When I’m lower, it all feels more obvious to me, like I can see the pattern clearer, even if other people tell me I’m reading too much into it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I can say for sure the depression came before the apartment problems. I just know when the depression gets bad, that’s when the apartment stuff feels the most real and the most intense.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the apartment concerns were present even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, there have been times like that. That’s part of what makes it hard for me when people say it’s only because I’m depressed, because there was a stretch — not a long, perfect stretch, but several weeks — where I was doing a little better. I was eating more normal, or at least better than I had been, and I wasn’t waking up with that same heavy hopeless feeling right away every morning.\n\nBut even then, I still felt like the neighbors were watching my door. I’d still notice when people were out in the hallway, or when things seemed to happen right after I left or came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like feeling a little better made that go away. Maybe I wasn’t as crushed down inside during that time, but I still thought the apartment stuff was happening.\n\nDoctor: When you’re feeling depressed, do you ever believe that you’re to blame for bad things happening, even when other people say that isn’t true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it exactly like I’m to blame for bad things happening. It’s more like… when I’m depressed, I get even more stuck on what’s going on at my apartment. That’s really why I came in, because I know the people there are watching when I leave and when I come back, like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings.\n\nAnd then there are these notes — people keep telling me, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like regular apartment notices, but it doesn’t feel like that to me. It feels like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like they’re passing along information. Everybody says I’m making too much of it, but I can’t just ignore it when it keeps happening.\n\nSo… I don’t know about being the cause of bad things, exactly. It’s more that I feel targeted, like they’re watching me and reporting on me, even when other people keep saying it isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were sure the people at your apartment were watching or reporting on you, but you were not feeling depressed during that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was. I mean, I can’t give you exact dates like on a calendar, because I don’t keep track that neatly, but there was a spell that went on for several weeks where I wasn’t as depressed as I had been.\n\nLike, I was eating a little better — not perfect or anything, but more regular — and I wasn’t waking up every morning with that same hopeless feeling sitting on my chest right away. I could get up a little easier, do a few things, maybe answer the phone once in a while.\n\nBut the apartment thing didn’t stop during that time. I still felt sure the neighbors were watching my door, watching when I left and came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like my mood got a little lighter and then I suddenly thought, “Oh, that’s nothing.” I still thought it was happening. So yes, I’d say it lasted at least a couple weeks, probably several weeks, even when I wasn’t feeling that depressed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I don’t think it’s like someone moving my arms or making my legs walk, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t really felt like a remote control kind of thing.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because things at my apartment have gotten… I don’t know, it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making a big deal out of it, but I know people there are watching when I come and go. I can get short with people, or I’ll feel on edge, but I don’t know if that’s “unusual” exactly.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is. I don’t have times where I feel on top of the world or like I don’t need to slow down. Mostly it’s more like I’m bothered, tense, worn out. That sounds… I don’t know, like a very specific thing. What I do know is I came in because something is going on at my apartment, and everybody keeps trying to make it sound like it’s nothing.\n\nThe people there are watching when I leave and when I come back. I can tell because it’s too lined up, you know? Like I’ll go out, and then there’ll be notes on doors, or something slipped around, and they say, “Oh, those are just notices from the office,” like it’s normal building stuff. But it’s not just that. It feels like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings and then passing notes about me to the office, and then when I say it, people act like I’m making too much of it.\n\nSo… thoughts being put in my mind, no, I wouldn’t describe it that way. But I do feel like other people are involved in what’s happening around me, and they know more about me than they should.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences at your apartment been going on?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about a year, a little more than a year. Maybe around a year and a month, if I’m trying to place it right.\n\nIt didn’t all come on at once like one big event. At first it was just little things I noticed, and I thought maybe I was just being careful or maybe the building was busy. But then it kept lining up, over and over, with the neighbors and the office, and I started realizing it wasn’t just a bad week or something.\n\nSo yeah… these thoughts about the apartment, people watching and passing things along, that’s been going on for about a year and a month now.\n\nDoctor: When the depression first started, was it before the problems at your apartment began?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, like, “this started first and then that started,” because in my head they kind of feed into each other. But what I notice is when I’m really down — like in that hollow place I was talking about, where I’m barely eating, not really picking up the phone, just sitting there with the curtains closed and not wanting to deal with anybody — that’s when I’m also the most sure about the apartment people.\n\nLike, those are the times I feel it the strongest, that they’re tracking when I come and go, talking to the office, keeping notes or whatever they’re doing. When I’m lower, it all feels more obvious to me, like I can see the pattern clearer, even if other people tell me I’m reading too much into it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I can say for sure the depression came before the apartment problems. I just know when the depression gets bad, that’s when the apartment stuff feels the most real and the most intense.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the apartment concerns were present even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, there have been times like that. That’s part of what makes it hard for me when people say it’s only because I’m depressed, because there was a stretch — not a long, perfect stretch, but several weeks — where I was doing a little better. I was eating more normal, or at least better than I had been, and I wasn’t waking up with that same heavy hopeless feeling right away every morning.\n\nBut even then, I still felt like the neighbors were watching my door. I’d still notice when people were out in the hallway, or when things seemed to happen right after I left or came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like feeling a little better made that go away. Maybe I wasn’t as crushed down inside during that time, but I still thought the apartment stuff was happening.\n\nDoctor: When you’re feeling depressed, do you ever believe that you’re to blame for bad things happening, even when other people say that isn’t true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it exactly like I’m to blame for bad things happening. It’s more like… when I’m depressed, I get even more stuck on what’s going on at my apartment. That’s really why I came in, because I know the people there are watching when I leave and when I come back, like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings.\n\nAnd then there are these notes — people keep telling me, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like regular apartment notices, but it doesn’t feel like that to me. It feels like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like they’re passing along information. Everybody says I’m making too much of it, but I can’t just ignore it when it keeps happening.\n\nSo… I don’t know about being the cause of bad things, exactly. It’s more that I feel targeted, like they’re watching me and reporting on me, even when other people keep saying it isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were sure the people at your apartment were watching or reporting on you, but you were not feeling depressed during that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was. I mean, I can’t give you exact dates like on a calendar, because I don’t keep track that neatly, but there was a spell that went on for several weeks where I wasn’t as depressed as I had been.\n\nLike, I was eating a little better — not perfect or anything, but more regular — and I wasn’t waking up every morning with that same hopeless feeling sitting on my chest right away. I could get up a little easier, do a few things, maybe answer the phone once in a while.\n\nBut the apartment thing didn’t stop during that time. I still felt sure the neighbors were watching my door, watching when I left and came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like my mood got a little lighter and then I suddenly thought, “Oh, that’s nothing.” I still thought it was happening. So yes, I’d say it lasted at least a couple weeks, probably several weeks, even when I wasn’t feeling that depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that outside forces were controlling your actions or movements?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like someone moving my arms or making my legs walk, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t really felt like a remote control kind of thing.\n\nIt’s more… the reason I came in is because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. Like they’re keeping track of me, my comings and goings. And then those notes on the doors — everyone keeps saying, “Those are just regular notices,” but it doesn’t feel regular to me. It feels like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like reporting on what I’m doing.\n\nSo not controlling my movements exactly, no. More like watching them and keeping records of them, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

I mean, I can’t give you exact dates like on a calendar, because I don’t keep track that neatly, but there was a spell that went on for several weeks where I wasn’t as depressed as I had been.\n\nLike, I was eating a little better — not perfect or anything, but more regular — and I wasn’t waking up every morning with that same hopeless feeling sitting on my chest right away. I could get up a little easier, do a few things, maybe answer the phone once in a while.\n\nBut the apartment thing didn’t stop during that time. I still felt sure the neighbors were watching my door, watching when I left and came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like my mood got a little lighter and then I suddenly thought, “Oh, that’s nothing.” I still thought it was happening. So yes, I’d say it lasted at least a couple weeks, probably several weeks, even when I wasn’t feeling that depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that outside forces were controlling your actions or movements?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like someone moving my arms or making my legs walk, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t really felt like a remote control kind of thing.\n\nIt’s more… the reason I came in is because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. Like they’re keeping track of me, my comings and goings. And then those notes on the doors — everyone keeps saying, “Those are just regular notices,” but it doesn’t feel regular to me. It feels like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like reporting on what I’m doing.\n\nSo not controlling my movements exactly, no. More like watching them and keeping records of them, if that makes sense.\n\n[Last Question]\nDuring those depressed periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 59 / not_in_workforce / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Loss_of_Interest\nmanifestation: \"I used to look forward to choir practice, bridge, cooking beans on Sunday, and doing little repair jobs for folks, but now I mostly let the phone ring and tell myself there is no point going.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yes, I did. That’s actually one of the things that made me feel like something was really wrong, because I used to have things I looked forward to, even if they were small.

Like choir practice — I used to go, and even if I wasn’t the best singer in the room, I liked being there with people, knowing the songs, having somewhere to be. And bridge, too. I used to enjoy sitting down for a game, keeping my mind busy, talking a little. Sundays I’d make beans, real slow, the way I like them, and it felt like a routine, something steady. And I used to do little repair jobs for folks, you know, fixing this or tightening that, small things, but it made me feel useful.

But when I’m depressed, I don’t feel that pull toward any of it. The phone rings and I mostly just let it ring. I’ll see who it is and think, “I should answer,” but then I just don’t. Or I tell myself there’s no point going, no point cooking, no point showing up, because I won’t enjoy it anyway and I’ll just be sitting there pretending. So, yes… I lose interest in things I normally care about.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F20.0, F25.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because things at my apartment have gotten… I don’t know, it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making a big deal out of it, but I know people there are watching when I come and go. Like, they notice what time I leave, what time I come back, who I talk to, that kind of thing. And then there’s these notes, or papers, on the doors and around the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Oh, those are just normal notices,” like building notices or whatever, but it doesn’t feel normal to me.\n\nIt feels like some of them are about me, or that people are slipping notes to the office about me. Like they’re keeping track. I can tell by the way things show up after I’ve done something, or how people act when I walk by. I know they’ll say it’s nothing, but it’s been bothering me enough that I figured I better come talk to somebody before it gets worse or before they twist it around more.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think so in the way people usually mean it. Like, I’m not sitting in my apartment hearing somebody talking clear as day when nobody’s there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I hear noises in the building, people in the hallway, doors, voices through the walls maybe, but it’s hard to tell in apartments because sound carries weird. So I don’t want to say “voices” like it’s coming out of nowhere, because I’m not sure that’s what’s happening. It’s more that I notice things and I wonder what people are saying, but as far as hearing a voice when no one else is around… no, not really that I can say.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was unusually high or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Unusually high… no, I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not really the type to get all energized or excited like that, if that’s what you mean. Irritable, maybe sometimes, but I mean, who wouldn’t be when things feel off around you or people are acting strange? I can get short with people, or I’ll feel on edge, but I don’t know if that’s “unusual” exactly.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is. I don’t have times where I feel on top of the world or like I don’t need to slow down. Mostly it’s more like I’m bothered, tense, worn out. If I seem irritable, it’s usually because something’s been building up and I’m trying to figure out what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. Most days it’s there, especially in the morning. I wake up and before I even really do anything, before I even get my feet on the floor, there’s this heavy kind of hollow feeling. Like the day already went bad somehow, even though it just started.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because it’s not always like I’m crying or anything dramatic. It’s more like this weight sitting in me, and I’m already tired of the day before I’ve even started it. Some mornings I just lie there for a bit, thinking, “Here we go again,” and it feels like I’m dragging myself into the day instead of just getting up like a normal person. So, yeah… depressed most of the day, a lot of days, that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Have you had the feeling that your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it like that, like somebody is putting thoughts inside my head. That sounds… I don’t know, like a very specific thing. What I do know is I came in because something is going on at my apartment, and everybody keeps trying to make it sound like it’s nothing.\n\nThe people there are watching when I leave and when I come back. I can tell because it’s too lined up, you know? Like I’ll go out, and then there’ll be notes on doors, or something slipped around, and they say, “Oh, those are just notices from the office,” like it’s normal building stuff. But it’s not just that. It feels like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings and then passing notes about me to the office, and then when I say it, people act like I’m making too much of it.\n\nSo… thoughts being put in my mind, no, I wouldn’t describe it that way. But I do feel like other people are involved in what’s happening around me, and they know more about me than they should.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences at your apartment been going on?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about a year, a little more than a year. Maybe around a year and a month, if I’m trying to place it right.\n\nIt didn’t all come on at once like one big event. At first it was just little things I noticed, and I thought maybe I was just being careful or maybe the building was busy. But then it kept lining up, over and over, with the neighbors and the office, and I started realizing it wasn’t just a bad week or something.\n\nSo yeah… these thoughts about the apartment, people watching and passing things along, that’s been going on for about a year and a month now.\n\nDoctor: When the depression first started, was it before the problems at your apartment began?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, like, “this started first and then that started,” because in my head they kind of feed into each other. But what I notice is when I’m really down — like in that hollow place I was talking about, where I’m barely eating, not really picking up the phone, just sitting there with the curtains closed and not wanting to deal with anybody — that’s when I’m also the most sure about the apartment people.\n\nLike, those are the times I feel it the strongest, that they’re tracking when I come and go, talking to the office, keeping notes or whatever they’re doing. When I’m lower, it all feels more obvious to me, like I can see the pattern clearer, even if other people tell me I’m reading too much into it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I can say for sure the depression came before the apartment problems. I just know when the depression gets bad, that’s when the apartment stuff feels the most real and the most intense.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the apartment concerns were present even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, there have been times like that. That’s part of what makes it hard for me when people say it’s only because I’m depressed, because there was a stretch — not a long, perfect stretch, but several weeks — where I was doing a little better. I was eating more normal, or at least better than I had been, and I wasn’t waking up with that same heavy hopeless feeling right away every morning.\n\nBut even then, I still felt like the neighbors were watching my door. I’d still notice when people were out in the hallway, or when things seemed to happen right after I left or came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like feeling a little better made that go away. Maybe I wasn’t as crushed down inside during that time, but I still thought the apartment stuff was happening.\n\nDoctor: When you’re feeling depressed, do you ever believe that you’re to blame for bad things happening, even when other people say that isn’t true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it exactly like I’m to blame for bad things happening. It’s more like… when I’m depressed, I get even more stuck on what’s going on at my apartment. That’s really why I came in, because I know the people there are watching when I leave and when I come back, like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings.\n\nAnd then there are these notes — people keep telling me, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like regular apartment notices, but it doesn’t feel like that to me. It feels like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like they’re passing along information. Everybody says I’m making too much of it, but I can’t just ignore it when it keeps happening.\n\nSo… I don’t know about being the cause of bad things, exactly. It’s more that I feel targeted, like they’re watching me and reporting on me, even when other people keep saying it isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were sure the people at your apartment were watching or reporting on you, but you were not feeling depressed during that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… people have said that to me. Not everybody, but enough that I notice it.

It’s usually when I’m trying to explain something that, in my head, all connects. Like I’ll start talking about the rent, because that’s where the problem began in my mind, and then I’ll go to the marina because that’s part of how I understood where things were being passed around, and then somehow I’m talking about the choir robes or the wires in the walls, and people just look at me like I skipped half the story.

I don’t feel like I’m skipping it when I’m saying it. It feels like I’m following the line from one thing to the next, but other people will stop me and say, “Wait, what are you talking about?” or “Start over.” Sometimes they get impatient, like I’m rambling or mixing things up on purpose, but I’m not. I’m trying to get it out the right way. It’s just hard to explain all the pieces without going into the whole thing.

From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF25.1, F20.0, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, I’m glad you came in. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because things at my apartment have gotten… I don’t know, it’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making a big deal out of it, but I know people there are watching when I come and go. Like, they notice what time I leave, what time I come back, who I talk to, that kind of thing. And then there’s these notes, or papers, on the doors and around the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Oh, those are just normal notices,” like building notices or whatever, but it doesn’t feel normal to me.\n\nIt feels like some of them are about me, or that people are slipping notes to the office about me. Like they’re keeping track. I can tell by the way things show up after I’ve done something, or how people act when I walk by. I know they’ll say it’s nothing, but it’s been bothering me enough that I figured I better come talk to somebody before it gets worse or before they twist it around more.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I don’t think so in the way people usually mean it. Like, I’m not sitting in my apartment hearing somebody talking clear as day when nobody’s there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I hear noises in the building, people in the hallway, doors, voices through the walls maybe, but it’s hard to tell in apartments because sound carries weird. So I don’t want to say “voices” like it’s coming out of nowhere, because I’m not sure that’s what’s happening. It’s more that I notice things and I wonder what people are saying, but as far as hearing a voice when no one else is around… no, not really that I can say.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was unusually high or unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Unusually high… no, I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not really the type to get all energized or excited like that, if that’s what you mean. Irritable, maybe sometimes, but I mean, who wouldn’t be when things feel off around you or people are acting strange? I can get short with people, or I’ll feel on edge, but I don’t know if that’s “unusual” exactly.\n\nI guess I’m not sure where the line is. I don’t have times where I feel on top of the world or like I don’t need to slow down. Mostly it’s more like I’m bothered, tense, worn out. If I seem irritable, it’s usually because something’s been building up and I’m trying to figure out what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. Most days it’s there, especially in the morning. I wake up and before I even really do anything, before I even get my feet on the floor, there’s this heavy kind of hollow feeling. Like the day already went bad somehow, even though it just started.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because it’s not always like I’m crying or anything dramatic. It’s more like this weight sitting in me, and I’m already tired of the day before I’ve even started it. Some mornings I just lie there for a bit, thinking, “Here we go again,” and it feels like I’m dragging myself into the day instead of just getting up like a normal person. So, yeah… depressed most of the day, a lot of days, that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Have you had the feeling that your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it like that, like somebody is putting thoughts inside my head. That sounds… I don’t know, like a very specific thing. What I do know is I came in because something is going on at my apartment, and everybody keeps trying to make it sound like it’s nothing.\n\nThe people there are watching when I leave and when I come back. I can tell because it’s too lined up, you know? Like I’ll go out, and then there’ll be notes on doors, or something slipped around, and they say, “Oh, those are just notices from the office,” like it’s normal building stuff. But it’s not just that. It feels like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings and then passing notes about me to the office, and then when I say it, people act like I’m making too much of it.\n\nSo… thoughts being put in my mind, no, I wouldn’t describe it that way. But I do feel like other people are involved in what’s happening around me, and they know more about me than they should.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences at your apartment been going on?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about a year, a little more than a year. Maybe around a year and a month, if I’m trying to place it right.\n\nIt didn’t all come on at once like one big event. At first it was just little things I noticed, and I thought maybe I was just being careful or maybe the building was busy. But then it kept lining up, over and over, with the neighbors and the office, and I started realizing it wasn’t just a bad week or something.\n\nSo yeah… these thoughts about the apartment, people watching and passing things along, that’s been going on for about a year and a month now.\n\nDoctor: When the depression first started, was it before the problems at your apartment began?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, like, “this started first and then that started,” because in my head they kind of feed into each other. But what I notice is when I’m really down — like in that hollow place I was talking about, where I’m barely eating, not really picking up the phone, just sitting there with the curtains closed and not wanting to deal with anybody — that’s when I’m also the most sure about the apartment people.\n\nLike, those are the times I feel it the strongest, that they’re tracking when I come and go, talking to the office, keeping notes or whatever they’re doing. When I’m lower, it all feels more obvious to me, like I can see the pattern clearer, even if other people tell me I’m reading too much into it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I can say for sure the depression came before the apartment problems. I just know when the depression gets bad, that’s when the apartment stuff feels the most real and the most intense.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the apartment concerns were present even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, there have been times like that. That’s part of what makes it hard for me when people say it’s only because I’m depressed, because there was a stretch — not a long, perfect stretch, but several weeks — where I was doing a little better. I was eating more normal, or at least better than I had been, and I wasn’t waking up with that same heavy hopeless feeling right away every morning.\n\nBut even then, I still felt like the neighbors were watching my door. I’d still notice when people were out in the hallway, or when things seemed to happen right after I left or came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like feeling a little better made that go away. Maybe I wasn’t as crushed down inside during that time, but I still thought the apartment stuff was happening.\n\nDoctor: When you’re feeling depressed, do you ever believe that you’re to blame for bad things happening, even when other people say that isn’t true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it exactly like I’m to blame for bad things happening. It’s more like… when I’m depressed, I get even more stuck on what’s going on at my apartment. That’s really why I came in, because I know the people there are watching when I leave and when I come back, like they’re keeping track of my comings and goings.\n\nAnd then there are these notes — people keep telling me, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like regular apartment notices, but it doesn’t feel like that to me. It feels like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like they’re passing along information. Everybody says I’m making too much of it, but I can’t just ignore it when it keeps happening.\n\nSo… I don’t know about being the cause of bad things, exactly. It’s more that I feel targeted, like they’re watching me and reporting on me, even when other people keep saying it isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were sure the people at your apartment were watching or reporting on you, but you were not feeling depressed during that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was. I mean, I can’t give you exact dates like on a calendar, because I don’t keep track that neatly, but there was a spell that went on for several weeks where I wasn’t as depressed as I had been.\n\nLike, I was eating a little better — not perfect or anything, but more regular — and I wasn’t waking up every morning with that same hopeless feeling sitting on my chest right away. I could get up a little easier, do a few things, maybe answer the phone once in a while.\n\nBut the apartment thing didn’t stop during that time. I still felt sure the neighbors were watching my door, watching when I left and came back, and I still believed they were reporting on me to the office. It wasn’t like my mood got a little lighter and then I suddenly thought, “Oh, that’s nothing.” I still thought it was happening. So yes, I’d say it lasted at least a couple weeks, probably several weeks, even when I wasn’t feeling that depressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that outside forces were controlling your actions or movements?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like someone moving my arms or making my legs walk, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t really felt like a remote control kind of thing.\n\nIt’s more… the reason I came in is because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. Like they’re keeping track of me, my comings and goings. And then those notes on the doors — everyone keeps saying, “Those are just regular notices,” but it doesn’t feel regular to me. It feels like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like reporting on what I’m doing.\n\nSo not controlling my movements exactly, no. More like watching them and keeping records of them, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: During those depressed periods, did you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I did. That’s actually one of the things that made me feel like something was really wrong, because I used to have things I looked forward to, even if they were small.\n\nLike choir practice — I used to go, and even if I wasn’t the best singer in the room, I liked being there with people, knowing the songs, having somewhere to be. And bridge, too. I used to enjoy sitting down for a game, keeping my mind busy, talking a little. Sundays I’d make beans, real slow, the way I like them, and it felt like a routine, something steady. And I used to do little repair jobs for folks, you know, fixing this or tightening that, small things, but it made me feel useful.\n\nBut when I’m depressed, I don’t feel that pull toward any of it. The phone rings and I mostly just let it ring. I’ll see who it is and think, “I should answer,” but then I just don’t. Or I tell myself there’s no point going, no point cooking, no point showing up, because I won’t enjoy it anyway and I’ll just be sitting there pretending. So, yes… I lose interest in things I normally care about.\n\nDoctor: Have people ever told you that your speech or thoughts were hard to follow or seemed mixed up?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have said that to me. Not everybody, but enough that I notice it.\n\nIt’s usually when I’m trying to explain something that, in my head, all connects. Like I’ll start talking about the rent, because that’s where the problem began in my mind, and then I’ll go to the marina because that’s part of how I understood where things were being passed around, and then somehow I’m talking about the choir robes or the wires in the walls, and people just look at me like I skipped half the story.\n\nI don’t feel like I’m skipping it when I’m saying it. It feels like I’m following the line from one thing to the next, but other people will stop me and say, “Wait, what are you talking about?” or “Start over.” Sometimes they get impatient, like I’m rambling or mixing things up on purpose, but I’m not. I’m trying to get it out the right way. It’s just hard to explain all the pieces without going into the whole thing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yes… I think so. There was a stretch, not forever, but several weeks where I wasn’t down in that heavy way. I was eating a little better, and I wasn’t waking up with that same hopeless feeling right away, like the day was already ruined before I even got out of bed.

Because I’d still be trying to explain about the neighbors watching my door — how they seemed to notice when I left and came back, and how it felt like they were reporting things about me. And I know people wanted me to just drop it because I wasn’t as depressed then, like they thought that should make the whole thing go away, but it didn’t.

So yes, there was a time when my mood was a bit better, but I still had those thoughts about being watched and reported on, and when I tried to explain it, people still acted like I was mixing things up or not making it clear.
